{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/itemModel.ts",
  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\nimport { HELD_BLOCK_COLORS, HELD_BLOCK_FALLBACK_COLOR } from \"@/lib/world\";\nimport type { InventorySlot } from \"@/lib/game/types\";\nimport { renderSpritePixels } from \"@/lib/ui/spritePixels\";\nimport { buildExtrudedSpriteGeometry } from \"./extrudedSprite\";\n\nexport type ItemModel = {\n  object: THREE.Object3D;\n  geometry: THREE.BufferGeometry;\n  material: THREE.Material;\n};\n\nconst blockColor = (blockId: number | undefined): number =>\n  (blockId !== undefined ? HELD_BLOCK_COLORS[blockId as keyof typeof HELD_BLOCK_COLORS] : undefined) ?? HELD_BLOCK_FALLBACK_COLOR;\n\n/**\n * Builds the 3D model for an inventory item — shared between the first-person\n * held-item overlay and the third-person hand. The caller owns disposal of the\n * returned geometry and material. Returns null for empty/invalid slots.\n */\nexport function buildItemModel(slot: InventorySlot | undefined): ItemModel | null {\n  if (!slot?.id || slot.count <= 0 || !slot.kind) return null;\n\n  if (slot.kind === \"block\") {\n    const geometry = new THREE.BoxGeometry(0.22, 0.22, 0.22);\n    const material = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: blockColor(slot.blockId), roughness: 0.7, metalness: 0.05 });\n    return { object: new THREE.Mesh(geometry, material), geometry, material };\n  }\n\n  // Tools, weapons, food, armor: extrude the same 16x16 pixel grid the\n  // inventory icon uses, so the in-hand model always matches the sprite.\n  const geometry = buildExtrudedSpriteGeometry(renderSpritePixels(slot.id));\n  const material = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ vertexColors: true, roughness: 0.7, metalness: 0.05 });\n  return { object: new THREE.Mesh(geometry, material), geometry, material };\n}\n"
}

import { describe, expect, test } from "bun:test";
import * as THREE from "three";
import { createHeldItemView, type HeldItemFrame } from "@/lib/game/render/heldItem";
import { BASE_POSE, SWING_MS } from "@/lib/game/render/heldItemPose";
import { createEmptySlot, createSlot } from "@/lib/game/items";

function heldHolder(camera: THREE.Camera): THREE.Group {
  const root = camera.children[0] as THREE.Group;
  return root.children[0] as THREE.Group;
}

function frame(timeMs = 0, overrides: Partial<HeldItemFrame> = {}): HeldItemFrame {
  return { timeMs, miningActive: false, moveFactor: 0, ...overrides };
}

describe("heldItem", () => {
  test("builds a model for block, tool, and weapon slots", () => {
    const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera();
    const view = createHeldItemView(camera);
    const holder = heldHolder(camera);

    view.update(createSlot("dirt", 5), frame());
    expect(holder.children).toHaveLength(1);

    view.update(createSlot("wood_pickaxe", 1), frame());
    expect(holder.children).toHaveLength(1);
    const tool = holder.children[0] as THREE.Mesh;
    expect((tool.material as THREE.MeshStandardMaterial).vertexColors).toBe(true);
    expect(tool.geometry.getAttribute("position").count).toBeGreaterThan(0);

    view.update(createSlot("knife", 1), frame());
    expect(holder.children).toHaveLength(1);
    view.dispose();
  });

  test("keeps the same model while the item id is unchanged, swaps when it changes", () => {
    const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera();
    const view = createHeldItemView(camera);
    const holder = heldHolder(camera);

1	/**
2	 * Pure pose math for the first-person held item. No Three.js — heldItem.ts
3	 * applies the result to the holder group each frame. All motion is a function
4	 * of wall-clock timeMs plus a few timestamps, so poses are deterministic and
5	 * testable headlessly.
6	 */
7	
8	export type HeldPose = {
9	  posX: number;
10	  posY: number;
11	  posZ: number;
12	  rotX: number;
13	  rotY: number;
14	  rotZ: number;
15	};
16	
17	/** Rest pose in camera space — lower-right of the view, matching the old static placement. */
18	export const BASE_POSE: HeldPose = { posX: 0.34, posY: -0.28, posZ: -0.55, rotX: -0.35, rotY: -0.55, rotZ: -0.12 };
19	
20	export const SWING_MS = 260;
21	export const EQUIP_MS = 220;
22	
23	export type HeldPoseInput = {
24	  timeMs: number;
25	  /** Start of the most recent swing; -Infinity when the item has never swung. */
26	  swingStartMs: number;
27	  /** True while mining — the swing arc loops instead of stopping after one cycle. */
28	  continuousSwing: boolean;
29	  /** Start of the most recent equip (item switch); -Infinity to skip the dip. */
30	  equipStartMs: number;
31	  /** 0..1 horizontal speed relative to walk speed — scales the walk bob. */
32	  moveFactor: number;
33	};
34	
35	const clamp01 = (v: number) => Math.max(0, Math.min(1, v));
36	
37	export function computeHeldPose(input: HeldPoseInput): HeldPose {
38	  const pose = { ...BASE_POSE };
39	
40	  // Swing: a sin(t*PI) chop arc — out and down, with a slight inward twist.
41	  let t = (input.timeMs - input.swingStartMs) / SWING_MS;
42	  if (input.continuousSwing && t >= 0) t %= 1;
43	  if (t >= 0 && t <= 1) {
44	    const s = Math.sin(t * Math.PI);
45	    pose.rotX -= s * 1.15;
46	    pose.rotY += s * 0.35;
47	    pose.posZ -= s * 0.16;
48	    pose.posY -= s * 0.1;
49	  }
50	
51	  // Equip: the item rises into frame from below after a slot switch.
52	  const e = clamp01((input.timeMs - input.equipStartMs) / EQUIP_MS);
53	  const q = 1 - (1 - e) * (1 - e);
54	  pose.posY -= (1 - q) * 0.4;
55	  pose.rotX -= (1 - q) * 0.5;
56	
57	  // Idle sway: barely-visible drift so the item never looks frozen.
58	  pose.posY += Math.sin(input.timeMs * 0.0016) * 0.006;
59	  pose.rotZ += Math.sin(input.timeMs * 0.0013) * 0.015;
60	
61	  // Walk bob: figure-eight-ish sway scaled by horizontal speed.
62	  const p = input.timeMs * 0.012;
63	  pose.posX += Math.sin(p) * 0.02 * input.moveFactor;
64	  pose.posY -= Math.abs(Math.sin(p)) * 0.025 * input.moveFactor;
65	
66	  return pose;
67	}
68

1	import * as THREE from "three";
2	import { createMobModelForKind } from "@/lib/game/mobs";
3	import type { MobModel } from "@/lib/game/types";
4	import type { MobState } from "@/lib/game/engine/state";
5	
6	export type MobVisuals = {
7	  /** Creates/removes models to match the mob list and animates bob + gait. */
8	  sync(mobs: MobState[], timeMs: number): void;
9	  dispose(): void;
10	};
11	
12	export function createMobVisuals(scene: THREE.Scene): MobVisuals {
13	  const models = new Map<number, MobModel>();
14	  const seen = new Set<number>();
15	
16	  const removeModel = (id: number) => {
17	    const model = models.get(id);
18	    if (!model) return;
19	    scene.remove(model.group);
20	    for (const material of model.materials) material.dispose();
21	    for (const geometry of model.geometries) geometry.dispose();
22	    models.delete(id);
23	  };
24	
25	  return {
26	    sync(mobs, timeMs) {
27	      seen.clear();
28	      for (const mob of mobs) {
29	        seen.add(mob.id);
30	        let model = models.get(mob.id);
31	        if (!model) {
32	          model = createMobModelForKind(mob.kind);
33	          models.set(mob.id, model);
34	          scene.add(model.group);
35	        }
36	
37	        const bob = Math.sin(timeMs * 0.008 + mob.bobSeed) * 0.04;
38	        model.group.position.set(mob.position.x, mob.position.y + bob, mob.position.z);
39	        model.group.rotation.y = mob.yaw;
40	
41	        const gait = Math.sin(timeMs * 0.015 * mob.moveSpeed + mob.bobSeed) * 0.3;
42	        if (model.legs.length === 4) {
43	          model.legs[0].rotation.x = gait;
44	          model.legs[1].rotation.x = -gait;
45	          model.legs[2].rotation.x = -gait;
46	          model.legs[3].rotation.x = gait;
47	        }
48	      }
49	
50	      for (const id of models.keys()) {
51	        if (!seen.has(id)) removeModel(id);
52	      }
53	    },
54	
55	    dispose() {
56	      for (const id of [...models.keys()]) removeModel(id);
57	    }
58	  };
59	}
60

  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\n\n/**\n * The player's own body, visible only in third person. Box-mesh humanoid in\n * the same zero-asset style as the mobs, but with two arms and pivot groups at\n * the shoulders/hips so limbs swing about their joints instead of their\n * centers. The group origin sits at the feet and the face is built on the -Z\n * side, so `group.rotation.y = player.yaw` matches the camera convention\n * (forward at yaw 0 is -Z).\n */\nexport type PlayerModel = {\n  group: THREE.Group;\n  /** Neck pivot — apply the look pitch here so the body stays upright. */\n  head: THREE.Group;\n  leftArm: THREE.Group;\n  rightArm: THREE.Group;\n  leftLeg: THREE.Group;\n  rightLeg: THREE.Group;\n  /** Hand anchor inside the right arm — the held item model goes here. */\n  itemHolder: THREE.Group;\n  materials: THREE.Material[];\n  geometries: THREE.BufferGeometry[];\n};\n\nconst SKIN = 0xc68e63;\nconst HAIR = 0x4a3220;\nconst SHIRT = 0x2e8b83;\nconst PANTS = 0x3b3f8f;\nconst SHOES = 0x4a3527;\nconst EYE_WHITE = 0xffffff;\nconst EYE_PUPIL = 0x2b2b45;\n\nexport function createPlayerModel(): PlayerModel {\n  const group = new THREE.Group();\n  const materials: THREE.Material[] = [];\n  const geometries: THREE.BufferGeometry[] = [];\n\n  const material = (color: number) => {\n    const mat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color, roughness: 0.85, metalness: 0.02 });\n    materials.push(mat);\n    return mat;\n  };\n  const box = (w: number, h: number, d: number, mat: THREE.Material) => {\n    const geometry = new THREE.BoxGeometry(w, h, d);\n    geometries.push(geometry);\n    return new THREE.Mesh(geometry, mat);\n  };\n\n  const skinMat = material(SKIN);\n  const shirtMat = material(SHIRT);\n  const pantsMat = material(PANTS);\n  const shoeMat = material(SHOES);\n\n  // Legs: hip pivots at y=0.72, meshes hanging below (pants + shoe band).\n  const makeLeg = (x: number) => {\n    const pivot = new THREE.Group();\n    pivot.position.set(x, 0.72, 0);\n    const leg = box(0.2, 0.6, 0.2, pantsMat);\n    leg.position.y = -0.36;\n    const shoe = box(0.21, 0.12, 0.22, shoeMat);\n    shoe.position.set(0, -0.66, 0.005);\n    pivot.add(leg, shoe);\n    group.add(pivot);\n    return pivot;\n  };\n  const leftLeg = makeLeg(-0.11);\n  const rightLeg = makeLeg(0.11);\n\n  // Torso spans y 0.72–1.32.\n  const torso = box(0.46, 0.6, 0.24, shirtMat);\n  torso.position.y = 1.02;\n  group.add(torso);\n\n  // Arms: shoulder pivots just below the torso top; bare skin like Steve.\n  const makeArm = (x: number) => {\n    const pivot = new THREE.Group();\n    pivot.position.set(x, 1.26, 0);\n    const arm = box(0.16, 0.66, 0.16, skinMat);\n    arm.position.y = -0.33;\n    pivot.add(arm);\n    group.add(pivot);\n    return pivot;\n  };\n  const leftArm = makeArm(-0.31);\n  const rightArm = makeArm(0.31);\n\n  // Hand anchor at the end of the right arm, tilted slightly forward so held\n  // items read as carried rather than glued flat to the limb.\n  const itemHolder = new THREE.Group();\n  itemHolder.position.set(0, -0.62, -0.1);\n  itemHolder.rotation.x = -0.5;\n  itemHolder.scale.setScalar(0.8);\n  rightArm.add(itemHolder);\n\n  // Head on a neck pivot; the face (eyes) is on the -Z side.\n  const head = new THREE.Group();\n  head.position.set(0, 1.32, 0);\n  const skull = box(0.4, 0.4, 0.4, skinMat);\n  skull.position.y = 0.23;\n  const hair = box(0.42, 0.12, 0.42, material(HAIR));\n  hair.position.y = 0.4;\n  head.add(skull, hair);\n  const eyeWhite = material(EYE_WHITE);\n  const eyePupil = material(EYE_PUPIL);\n  for (const side of [-1, 1]) {\n    const white = box(0.09, 0.07, 0.02, eyeWhite);\n    white.position.set(side * 0.09, 0.26, -0.2);\n    const pupil = box(0.04, 0.07, 0.02, eyePupil);\n    pupil.position.set(side * 0.07, 0.26, -0.205);\n    head.add(white, pupil);\n  }\n  group.add(head);\n\n  return { group, head, leftArm, rightArm, leftLeg, rightLeg, itemHolder, materials, geometries };\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerPose.ts",
  "content": "/**\n * Pure pose math for the third-person player body. No Three.js —\n * playerVisuals.ts applies the rotation.x values to the limb pivots each\n * frame. Deterministic in timeMs, like heldItemPose.ts.\n */\n\nexport const PLAYER_SWING_MS = 260;\n\nexport type PlayerPoseInput = {\n  timeMs: number;\n  /** 0..1 horizontal speed relative to walk speed — scales the walk gait. */\n  moveFactor: number;\n  /** True while mining — the arm chop loops instead of stopping after one cycle. */\n  miningActive: boolean;\n  /** Start of the most recent swing; -Infinity when the player has never swung. */\n  swingStartMs: number;\n};\n\n/** rotation.x per limb pivot; negative swings the limb forward (toward -Z). */\nexport type PlayerPose = {\n  leftArmX: number;\n  rightArmX: number;\n  leftLegX: number;\n  rightLegX: number;\n};\n\nexport function computePlayerPose(input: PlayerPoseInput): PlayerPose {\n  // Walk gait: legs counter-phased, each arm counter-phased to its same-side\n  // leg, all scaled by horizontal speed so the body idles when standing.\n  const gait = Math.sin(input.timeMs * 0.012) * 0.8 * input.moveFactor;\n  const pose = {\n    leftLegX: gait,\n    rightLegX: -gait,\n    leftArmX: -gait * 0.8,\n    rightArmX: gait * 0.8\n  };\n\n  // Attack/mining chop: the right arm raises forward and falls back, matching\n  // the first-person held-item swing timing.\n  let t = (input.timeMs - input.swingStartMs) / PLAYER_SWING_MS;\n  if (input.miningActive && t >= 0) t %= 1;\n  if (t >= 0 && t <= 1) pose.rightArmX -= Math.sin(t * Math.PI) * 1.9;\n\n  return pose;\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerVisuals.ts",
  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\nimport { WALK_SPEED } from \"@/lib/game/config\";\nimport type { CameraMode } from \"@/lib/game/engine/state\";\nimport type { InventorySlot } from \"@/lib/game/types\";\nimport { buildItemModel, type ItemModel } from \"./itemModel\";\nimport { createPlayerModel } from \"./playerModel\";\nimport { computePlayerPose } from \"./playerPose\";\n\n/** The slice of GameState the player body needs — narrow so tests stay light. */\nexport type PlayerVisualsState = {\n  cameraMode: CameraMode;\n  isDead: boolean;\n  player: {\n    position: THREE.Vector3;\n    velocity: THREE.Vector3;\n    yaw: number;\n    pitch: number;\n    onGround: boolean;\n  };\n  inventory: InventorySlot[];\n  selectedSlot: number;\n  mining: { targetKey: string };\n};\n\nexport type PlayerVisuals = {\n  /** Positions, animates, and shows/hides the body. Call once per frame. */\n  sync(state: PlayerVisualsState, timeMs: number): void;\n  /** Queues a one-shot arm swing (attack click); latched on the next sync. */\n  triggerSwing(): void;\n  dispose(): void;\n};\n\nexport function createPlayerVisuals(scene: THREE.Scene): PlayerVisuals {\n  const model = createPlayerModel();\n  model.group.visible = false;\n  scene.add(model.group);\n\n  let heldModel: ItemModel | null = null;\n  let heldKey = \"\";\n  let swingQueued = false;\n  let swingStartMs = -Infinity;\n  let wasMining = false;\n\n  const clearHeldModel = () => {\n    if (!heldModel) return;\n    model.itemHolder.remove(heldModel.object);\n    heldModel.geometry.dispose();\n    heldModel.material.dispose();\n    heldModel = null;\n    heldKey = \"\";\n  };\n\n  const syncHeldItem = (slot: InventorySlot | undefined) => {\n    const nextKey = slot?.id && slot.count > 0 && slot.kind ? slot.id : \"\";\n    if (nextKey === heldKey) return;\n    clearHeldModel();\n    const built = buildItemModel(slot);\n    if (built) {\n      heldModel = built;\n      heldKey = nextKey;\n      model.itemHolder.add(built.object);\n    }\n  };\n\n  return {\n    sync(state, timeMs) {\n      const visible = state.cameraMode !== \"first\" && !state.isDead;\n      model.group.visible = visible;\n      if (!visible) {\n        // Don't replay stale swings when the body next becomes visible.\n        swingQueued = false;\n        return;\n      }\n\n      const { player } = state;\n      model.group.position.copy(player.position);\n      model.group.rotation.y = player.yaw;\n      model.head.rotation.x = player.pitch;\n\n      syncHeldItem(state.inventory[state.selectedSlot]);\n\n      const miningActive = state.mining.targetKey !== \"\";\n      if (swingQueued) {\n        swingQueued = false;\n        swingStartMs = timeMs;\n      }\n      if (miningActive && !wasMining) swingStartMs = timeMs;\n      wasMining = miningActive;\n\n      const moveFactor = player.onGround ? Math.min(1, Math.hypot(player.velocity.x, player.velocity.z) / WALK_SPEED) : 0;\n      const pose = computePlayerPose({ timeMs, moveFactor, miningActive, swingStartMs });\n      model.leftArm.rotation.x = pose.leftArmX;\n      model.rightArm.rotation.x = pose.rightArmX;\n      model.leftLeg.rotation.x = pose.leftLegX;\n      model.rightLeg.rotation.x = pose.rightLegX;\n    },\n\n    triggerSwing() {\n      swingQueued = true;\n    },\n\n    dispose() {\n      clearHeldModel();\n      scene.remove(model.group);\n      for (const material of model.materials) material.dispose();\n      for (const geometry of model.geometries) geometry.dispose();\n    }\n  };\n}\n"
}

  "content": "import { describe, expect, test } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { computePlayerPose, PLAYER_SWING_MS } from \"@/lib/game/render/playerPose\";\n\nconst idle = { moveFactor: 0, miningActive: false, swingStartMs: -Infinity };\n\ndescribe(\"computePlayerPose\", () => {\n  test(\"idle body holds every limb at rest\", () => {\n    const pose = computePlayerPose({ timeMs: 1234, ...idle });\n    expect(pose.leftArmX).toBe(0);\n    expect(pose.rightArmX).toBe(0);\n    expect(pose.leftLegX).toBe(0);\n    expect(pose.rightLegX).toBe(0);\n  });\n\n  test(\"walking counter-phases the legs and opposes each arm to its leg\", () => {\n    // sin(t*0.012) peaks near t = pi/2 / 0.012.\n    const timeMs = (Math.PI / 2) / 0.012;\n    const pose = computePlayerPose({ timeMs, ...idle, moveFactor: 1 });\n    expect(pose.leftLegX).toBeGreaterThan(0.5);\n    expect(pose.rightLegX).toBeCloseTo(-pose.leftLegX);\n    expect(Math.sign(pose.leftArmX)).toBe(-Math.sign(pose.leftLegX));\n    expect(Math.sign(pose.rightArmX)).toBe(-Math.sign(pose.rightLegX));\n  });\n\n  test(\"gait scales with moveFactor\", () => {\n    const timeMs = (Math.PI / 2) / 0.012;\n    const slow = computePlayerPose({ timeMs, ...idle, moveFactor: 0.5 });\n    const fast = computePlayerPose({ timeMs, ...idle, moveFactor: 1 });\n    expect(slow.leftLegX).toBeCloseTo(fast.leftLegX / 2);\n  });\n\n  test(\"a swing raises the right arm forward, then returns to rest\", () => {\n    const mid = computePlayerPose({ timeMs: PLAYER_SWING_MS / 2, ...idle, swingStartMs: 0 });\n    expect(mid.rightArmX).toBeLessThan(-1);\n    const done = computePlayerPose({ timeMs: PLAYER_SWING_MS * 2, ...idle, swingStartMs: 0 });\n    expect(done.rightArmX).toBe(0);\n  });\n\n  test(\"mining loops the chop past the one-shot window\", () => {\n    const looped = computePlayerPose({ timeMs: PLAYER_SWING_MS * 2.5, ...idle, miningActive: true, swingStartMs: 0 });\n    expect(looped.rightArmX).toBeLessThan(-1);\n  });\n});\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerModel.test.ts",
  "content": "import { describe, expect, test } from \"bun:test\";\nimport * as THREE from \"three\";\nimport { createPlayerModel } from \"@/lib/game/render/playerModel\";\n\ndescribe(\"createPlayerModel\", () => {\n  test(\"builds a humanoid with pivoting limbs and a neck pivot\", () => {\n    const model = createPlayerModel();\n    for (const limb of [model.head, model.leftArm, model.rightArm, model.leftLeg, model.rightLeg]) {\n      expect(limb).toBeInstanceOf(THREE.Group);\n      expect(limb.parent).toBe(model.group);\n    }\n    // Pivots sit at the joints, not the limb centers.\n    expect(model.leftLeg.position.y).toBeCloseTo(0.72);\n    expect(model.leftArm.position.y).toBeCloseTo(1.26);\n    expect(model.head.position.y).toBeCloseTo(1.32);\n  });\n\n  test(\"the face is on the -Z side, matching the yaw-forward convention\", () => {\n    const model = createPlayerModel();\n    const eyes = model.head.children.filter((child) => (child as THREE.Mesh).position.z < -0.1);\n    expect(eyes.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);\n  });\n\n  test(\"the item holder hangs from the right arm\", () => {\n    const model = createPlayerModel();\n    expect(model.itemHolder.parent).toBe(model.rightArm);\n    expect(model.itemHolder.position.y).toBeLessThan(-0.5);\n  });\n\n  test(\"tracks every material and geometry for disposal\", () => {\n    const model = createPlayerModel();\n    const meshes: THREE.Mesh[] = [];\n    model.group.traverse((obj) => {\n      if (obj instanceof THREE.Mesh) meshes.push(obj);\n    });\n    expect(meshes.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(10);\n    for (const mesh of meshes) {\n      expect(model.geometries).toContain(mesh.geometry);\n      expect(model.materials).toContain(mesh.material as THREE.Material);\n    }\n  });\n});\n"
}

\"@/lib/game/render/playerVisuals\";\nimport { createEmptySlot, createSlot } from \"@/lib/game/items\";\n\nfunction makeState(overrides: Partial<PlayerVisualsState> = {}): PlayerVisualsState {\n  return {\n    cameraMode: \"third-rear\",\n    isDead: false,\n    player: {\n      position: new THREE.Vector3(10, 20, 30),\n      velocity: new THREE.Vector3(),\n      yaw: 0.7,\n      pitch: -0.2,\n      onGround: true\n    },\n    inventory: [createSlot(\"dirt\", 5)],\n    selectedSlot: 0,\n    mining: { targetKey: \"\" },\n    ...overrides\n  };\n}\n\nfunction bodyGroup(scene: THREE.Scene): THREE.Group {\n  return scene.children[0] as THREE.Group;\n}\n\ndescribe(\"playerVisuals\", () => {\n  test(\"hidden in first person, visible in both third-person modes\", () => {\n    const scene = new THREE.Scene();\n    const visuals = createPlayerVisuals(scene);\n    const group = bodyGroup(scene);\n\n    visuals.sync(makeState({ cameraMode: \"first\" }), 0);\n    expect(group.visible).toBe(false);\n    visuals.sync(makeState({ cameraMode: \"third-rear\" }), 0);\n    expect(group.visible).toBe(true);\n    visuals.sync(makeState({ cameraMode: \"third-front\" }), 0);\n    expect(group.visible).toBe(true);\n    visuals.dispose();\n  });\n\n  test(\"hidden while dead so the corpse never floats behind the death screen\", () => {\n    const scene = new THREE.Scene();\n    const visuals = createPlayerVisuals(scene);\n    visuals.sync(makeState({ isDead: true }), 0);\n    expect(bodyGroup(scene).visible).toBe(false);\n    visuals.dispose();\n  });\n\n  test(\"follows the player's feet position and yaw, pitch only tilts the head\", () => {\n    const scene = new THREE.Scene();\n    const visuals = createPlayerVisuals(scene);\n    const state = makeState();\n    visuals.sync(state, 0);\n\n    const group = bodyGroup(scene);\n    expect(group.position.toArray()).toEqual([10, 20, 30]);\n    expect(group.rotation.y).toBeCloseTo(0.7);\n    expect(group.rotation.x).toBe(0);\n    const head = group.children.find((child) => child.children.length > 2)!;\n    expect(head.rotation.x).toBeCloseTo(-0.2);\n    visuals.dispose();\n  });\n\n  test(\"rebuilds the hand item when the selected slot changes\", () => {\n    const scene = new THREE.Scene();\n    const visuals = createPlayerVisuals(scene);\n\n    const holderChildren = () => {\n      let holder: THREE.Object3D | null = null;\n      bodyGroup(scene).traverse((obj) => {\n        if (obj.name === \"\" && obj.parent && obj.scale.x === 0.8) holder = obj;\n      });\n      return (holder as THREE.Object3D | null)?.children.length ?? -1;\n    };\n\n    visuals.sync(makeState({ inventory: [createSlot(\"dirt\", 5)] }), 0);\n    expect(holderChildren()).toBe(1);\n    visuals.sync(makeState({ inventory: [createSlot(\"wood_pickaxe\", 1)] }), 16);\n    expect(holderChildren()).toBe(1);\n    visuals.sync(makeState({ inventory: [createEmptySlot()] }), 32);\n    expect(holderChildren()).toBe(0);\n    visuals.dispose();\n  });\n\n  test(\"a queued swing fires on the next visible sync, not after re-show\", () => {\n    const scene = new THREE.Scene();\n    const visuals = createPlayerVisuals(scene);\n    const group = bodyGroup(scene);\n    const rightArm = group.children.find((child) => child.children.length > 0 && child.position.x > 0.2)!;\n\n    // Swing queued while hidden is dropped.\n    visuals.triggerSwing();\n    visuals.sync(makeState({ cameraMode: \"first\" }), 0);\n    visuals.sync(makeState(), 130);\n    expect(rightArm.rotation.x).toBe(0);\n\n    // Swing queued while visible animates the arm at mid-swing.\n    visuals.triggerSwing();\n    visuals.sync(makeState(), 1000);\n    visuals.sync(makeState(), 1130);\n    expect(rightArm.rotation.x).toBeLessThan(-1);\n    visuals.dispose();\n  });\n\n  test(\"dispose removes the body from the scene\", () => {\n    const scene = new THREE.Scene();\n    const visuals = createPlayerVisuals(scene);\n    visuals.dispose();\n    expect(scene.children).toHaveLength(0);\n  });\n});\n"

46-  const { world, player, timers } = state;
// Gameplay tunables. Item/block data lives in items.ts, recipes in recipes.ts.

// Player physics
export const PLAYER_HEIGHT = 1.8;
export const PLAYER_HALF_WIDTH = 0.3;
export const EYE_HEIGHT = 1.62;
export const GRAVITY = 26;
export const JUMP_VELOCITY = 8.2;
export const WALK_SPEED = 4.8;
export const SPRINT_SPEED = 12.8;
export const CROUCH_SPEED = 2.1;
export const WORLD_BORDER_PADDING = 1.2;

// Player stats — Minecraft ranges: 20 HP shown as 10 hearts, 20 hunger as 10 drumsticks.
export const MAX_HEARTS = 20;
export const MAX_HUNGER = 20;
export const RESPAWN_SECONDS = 3;
export const HEALTH_REGEN_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 3;
// Health regen only runs at or above this hunger level; sprint needs more than SPRINT_MIN_HUNGER.
export const REGEN_MIN_HUNGER = 12;
export const SPRINT_MIN_HUNGER = 6;
// Hunger drain: one point per N blocks sprinted/walked, or per N jumps.
export const SPRINT_BLOCKS_PER_HUNGER = 100;
export const WALK_BLOCKS_PER_HUNGER = 300;
export const JUMPS_PER_HUNGER = 50;
export const FOOD_HUNGER = 7;

// Inventory
export const HOTBAR_SLOTS = 9;
export const INVENTORY_SLOTS = 36;
export const MAX_STACK_SIZE = 99;

// Mining & combat
export const MINE_REACH = 7;
export const MINING_RATE = 2.1; // progress per second per minePower
export const BARE_HAND_MINE_POWER = 0.8;
export const FIST_DAMAGE = 6;
export const ATTACK_REACH = 4.5;
export const ATTACK_AIM_DOT = 0.89; // how precisely the camera must face a mob

import * as THREE from "three";
import { buildGeometryRegion, createBlockAtlasTexture, VoxelWorld } from "@/lib/world";
import { EYE_HEIGHT, RENDER_GRID, RENDER_RADIUS, WALK_SPEED } from "@/lib/game/config";
import { sunAngleAt } from "@/lib/game/engine/systems/dayNight";
import type { GameState } from "@/lib/game/engine/state";
import { createCrackOverlay, type CrackOverlayView } from "./crackOverlay";
import { createHeldItemView, type HeldItemView } from "./heldItem";
import { createMobVisuals, type MobVisuals } from "./mobVisuals";

export type CreateRendererResult = { ok: true; renderer: GameRenderer } | { ok: false; error: string };

/**
 * Maps simulation state to Three.js every frame in sync(): camera from
 * yaw/pitch, world mesh from the voxel data (region-windowed, rebuilt when the
 * player crosses a RENDER_GRID boundary or state.worldMeshDirty is set), mob
 * models keyed by mob id, the held item, the crack overlay, and day-night
 * lighting. Owns every GPU resource and frees them in dispose().
 */
export class GameRenderer {
  private readonly mount: HTMLElement;
  private readonly scene: THREE.Scene;
  private readonly camera: THREE.PerspectiveCamera;
  private readonly webgl: THREE.WebGLRenderer;
  private readonly sun: THREE.DirectionalLight;
  private readonly hemiLight: THREE.HemisphereLight;
  private readonly daySky = new THREE.Color(0x8bc2ff);
  private readonly nightSky = new THREE.Color(0x06111f);
  private readonly liveSky = new THREE.Color(0x8bc2ff);
  private readonly worldMaterial: THREE.MeshStandardMaterial;
  private worldMesh: THREE.Mesh;
  private currentRegionX = Number.NaN;
  private currentRegionZ = Number.NaN;
  private readonly heldItem: HeldItemView;
  private readonly crackOverlay: CrackOverlayView;
  private readonly mobVisuals: MobVisuals;

  /** WebGL context creation can fail (blocked, unsupported) — surface it instead of throwing. */
  static create(mount: HTMLElement): CreateRendererResult {
    try {
      return { ok: true, renderer: new GameRenderer(mount) };
    } catch (error) {
      return { ok: false, error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : "Failed to initialize WebGL renderer" };
    }
  }

  private constructor(mount: HTMLElement) {
    this.mount = mount;
    this.scene = new THREE.Scene();
    this.scene.background = this.liveSky;
    this.scene.fog = new THREE.Fog(this.liveSky, 30, 200);
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/cameraView.ts",
  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\nimport type { CameraMode } from \"@/lib/game/engine/state\";\n\n/**\n * Pure camera placement math for the view modes. No world access — the\n * renderer raycasts for wall clamping and passes the final boom distance in.\n * Conventions match the rest of the engine: forward at yaw 0 is -Z, rotation\n * order YXZ, positive pitch looks up.\n */\n\nexport type CameraPose = {\n  posX: number;\n  posY: number;\n  posZ: number;\n  yaw: number;\n  pitch: number;\n};\n\n/** Unit direction from the eye toward the desired camera position. */\nexport function cameraOffsetDirection(mode: \"third-rear\" | \"third-front\", yaw: number, pitch: number, out: THREE.Vector3): THREE.Vector3 {\n  const cp = Math.cos(pitch);\n  // The player's look direction — same formula as lookDirection() in playerMotion.\n  out.set(-cp * Math.sin(yaw), Math.sin(pitch), -cp * Math.cos(yaw));\n  return mode === \"third-rear\" ? out.negate() : out;\n}\n\nexport function computeCameraPose(mode: CameraMode, eyeX: number, eyeY: number, eyeZ: number, yaw: number, pitch: number, distance: number, scratch: THREE.Vector3): CameraPose {\n  if (mode === \"first\") return { posX: eyeX, posY: eyeY, posZ: eyeZ, yaw, pitch };\n\n  const dir = cameraOffsetDirection(mode, yaw, pitch, scratch);\n  const posX = eyeX + dir.x * distance;\n  const posY = eyeY + dir.y * distance;\n  const posZ = eyeZ + dir.z * distance;\n\n  // Rear: over-the-shoulder, looking the same way as the player.\n  if (mode === \"third-rear\") return { posX, posY, posZ, yaw, pitch };\n  // Front: the camera faces back at the player, so the heading flips and the\n  // tilt inverts (player looks up -> the camera sits up-forward looking down).\n  return { posX, posY, posZ, yaw: yaw + Math.PI, pitch: -pitch };\n}\n"
}

  "new_string": "  private syncCamera(state: GameState): void {\n    const { position, yaw, pitch } = state.player;\n    scratchEye.set(position.x, position.y + EYE_HEIGHT, position.z);\n\n    let distance = 0;\n    if (state.cameraMode !== \"first\") {\n      // Clamp the boom against terrain so walls never occlude the player.\n      const dir = cameraOffsetDirection(state.cameraMode, yaw, pitch, scratchDir);\n      const hit = voxelRaycast(state.world, scratchEye, dir, THIRD_PERSON_DISTANCE);\n      distance = hit ? Math.max(0, hit.distance - THIRD_PERSON_MARGIN) : THIRD_PERSON_DISTANCE;\n    }\n\n    const pose = computeCameraPose(state.cameraMode, scratchEye.x, scratchEye.y, scratchEye.z, yaw, pitch, distance, scratchPose);\n    this.camera.position.set(pose.posX, pose.posY, pose.posZ);\n    this.camera.rotation.order = \"YXZ\";\n    this.camera.rotation.y = pose.yaw;\n    this.camera.rotation.x = pose.pitch;\n  }"
}

  // yaw 0, pitch 0: the player looks along -Z.\n    expect(cameraOffsetDirection(\"third-rear\", 0, 0, scratch).toArray()).toEqual([-0, -0, 1]);\n    expect(cameraOffsetDirection(\"third-front\", 0, 0, scratch).toArray()).toEqual([-0, 0, -1]);\n  });\n\n  test(\"looking up drops the rear camera below the look axis\", () => {\n    const dir = cameraOffsetDirection(\"third-rear\", 0, Math.PI / 4, scratch);\n    expect(dir.y).toBeLessThan(0);\n    expect(dir.z).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"computeCameraPose\", () => {\n  test(\"first person is the eye itself with passthrough rotation\", () => {\n    const pose = computeCameraPose(\"first\", 1, 2, 3, 0.5, -0.3, 99, scratch);\n    expect([pose.posX, pose.posY, pose.posZ]).toEqual([1, 2, 3]);\n    expect(pose.yaw).toBe(0.5);\n    expect(pose.pitch).toBe(-0.3);\n  });\n\n  test(\"rear sits behind the eye and keeps the player's view direction\", () => {\n    const pose = computeCameraPose(\"third-rear\", 0, 10, 0, 0, 0, 4, scratch);\n    expect(pose.posZ).toBeCloseTo(4);\n    expect(pose.posY).toBeCloseTo(10);\n    expect(pose.yaw).toBe(0);\n    expect(pose.pitch).toBe(0);\n  });\n\n  test(\"front sits ahead, flips the heading, and inverts the tilt\", () => {\n    const pose = computeCameraPose(\"third-front\", 0, 10, 0, 0.5, 0.4, 4, scratch);\n    const lookX = -Math.cos(0.4) * Math.sin(0.5) * 4;\n    const lookZ = -Math.cos(0.4) * Math.cos(0.5) * 4;\n    expect(pose.posX).toBeCloseTo(lookX);\n    expect(pose.posY).toBeCloseTo(10 + Math.sin(0.4) * 4);\n    expect(pose.posZ).toBeCloseTo(lookZ);\n    expect(pose.yaw).toBeCloseTo(0.5 + Math.PI);\n    expect(pose.pitch).toBeCloseTo(-0.4);\n  });\n\n  test(\"a clamped distance pulls the camera proportionally closer\", () => {\n    const far = computeCameraPose(\"third-rear\", 0, 10, 0, 0, 0, 4, scratch);\n    const near = computeCameraPose(\"third-rear\", 0, 10, 0, 0, 0, 1.5, scratch);\n    expect(near.posZ).toBeCloseTo((far.posZ * 1.5) / 4);\n  });\n});\n"
}

  test("switching items while mining does not carry the swing to the new item", () => {
    const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera();
    const view = createHeldItemView(camera);
    const holder = heldHolder(camera);

    view.update(createSlot("wood_pickaxe", 1), frame(-1000));
    view.update(createSlot("wood_pickaxe", 1), frame(0, { miningActive: true }));
    view.update(createSlot("knife", 1), frame(SWING_MS / 2)); // mining stopped by the switch
    expect(holder.position.z).toBeCloseTo(BASE_POSE.posZ);
    view.dispose();
  });

  test("equip dips the item after an item switch", () => {
    const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera();
    const view = createHeldItemView(camera);
    const holder = heldHolder(camera);

    view.update(createSlot("dirt", 5), frame(-1000));
    view.update(createSlot("knife", 1), frame(0)); // switch: equip starts now
    expect(holder.position.y).toBeCloseTo(BASE_POSE.posY - 0.4);
    view.dispose();
  });
});
```

# Monecraft (Next.js + TypeScript + Three.js + Bun)

[![CI](https://github.com/hutusi/monecraft/actions/workflows/ci.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/hutusi/monecraft/actions/workflows/ci.yml)

A playable Minecraft-inspired voxel game: procedural terrain with biomes, caves and ores, first-person movement, mining and building, crafting, armor, mobs, a day-night cycle, procedural sound and music (no audio files — everything is synthesized), and localStorage saves. Single-player, browser-only.

## Controls

- `W A S D`: Move · `Space`: Jump · `C`: Crouch
- `W + CapsLock`: Sprint (drains hunger)
- `Mouse`: Look around (click the game first to lock the pointer)
- `Left click` (hold): Break block / attack mobs
- `Right click` or `E`: Place selected block
- `1..9`: Select hotbar slot
- `I`: Inventory & crafting · `F`: Eat food · `U`: Emergency unstuck
- `Esc`: Pause menu (save / load / reset and the sound/music volume sliders live there) · `F3`: Debug overlay

## Run

```bash
bun install
bun run dev
```

Then open `http://localhost:3000`.

## Development

```bash
bun run lint        # ESLint
  onLoad: () => void;
  onReset: () => void;
};

const CONTROLS: Array<[string, string]> = [
  ["Move / Jump", "W A S D / Space"],
  ["Sprint / Crouch", "W + CapsLock / C"],
  ["Mine / Attack", "Hold left click / Left click"],
  ["Place block", "Right click or E"],
  ["Inventory", "I"],
  ["Eat food", "F"],
  ["Hotbar", "1-9"],
  ["Debug overlay", "F3"],
  ["Emergency unstuck", "U"]
];

/**
 * The Minecraft-style game menu: shown while the engine is paused, with the
 * classic column of gray beveled buttons over a dimmed (frozen) world.
 */
export default function PauseMenu({ saveMessage, audioSettings, onAudioSettingsChange, onBack, onSave, onLoad, onReset }: PauseMenuProps) {
# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

## [0.5.0] - 2026-06-13

### Added

- **Animated first-person held item**: a one-shot swing on attack clicks (hit or miss, via a new `attackSwung` engine event), a looping swing while mining, a walk bob scaled by movement speed, an equip dip when switching slots, and a faint idle sway
- Held tools, weapons, and food are now built by extruding their 16×16 inventory sprites into pixel-thick voxel meshes (one geometry, vertex colors), so the in-hand model always matches the icon; held blocks remain cubes
- Redesigned knife sprite: a single-edged drop-point blade with a bright cutting edge, dark spine, and riveted handle — clearly distinct from the swords (no crossguard). No save-format or worldgen impact
- **Procedural audio** — the game has sound, with zero audio assets (everything is synthesized at runtime, like the sprite system):
  - Block interaction SFX by material (stone/wood/grass/sand/glass/water): break, place, and staged mining hit ticks
  - Player feedback: surface-aware footsteps, jump and impact-scaled landing, hurt, eating, death/respawn stingers
  - Mob sounds: idle calls (sheep, chicken, horse, zombie, skeleton, spider) with distance falloff and look-relative stereo pan inside a 24-block earshot, plus attack and melee-hit sounds
  - Generative background music: a pentatonic ambient pad that brightens and quickens by day, darkens at night, and shifts with the biome (5 s hysteresis at borders); ducks behind the pause menu
  - Pause-menu Sound section: master and music sliders plus mute, persisted under a separate localStorage key (`minecraft_audio_v1`)
  - One-shot SFX ride on new engine `GameEvent`s (block broken/placed, hurt, ate, jumped, landed, mob attacked/hit); continuous sound derives from state, mirroring the renderer
  - New dependency: [zzfx](https://github.com/KilledByAPixel/ZzFX) (~1 KB, MIT) for SFX synthesis; the AudioContext is created only on the first user gesture (autoplay policy)
  - No save-format or worldgen impact

## [0.4.0] - 2026-06-12

### Added

- Snow and Cactus blocks (mineable, placeable; cactus deals no contact damage). Snow caps mountain tops above y=68; cacti scatter across dry desert sand
- Sand beaches where low land meets the sea (shoreline band around sea level)

- **Minecraft-style UI overhaul**: pixel-art hotbar with white selection outline and fading item-name popup, heart/hunger/armor icon rows, survival-layout inventory (armor column, 9×3 storage grid, hotbar row) with a visual recipe book (ingredient icons → result), pause menu (Esc) with Save/Load/Reset and a controls reference, red-tinted "You Died!" death screen with a Respawn button, and a toggleable F3 debug overlay (position, daylight, mob counts, FPS)
- Procedural pixel-art sprite system (`lib/ui/`): 16×16 item icons (isometric block cubes from `BLOCK_COLORS`, shape×material-palette tools/weapons/armor), HUD icons (hearts, drumsticks, armor), and UI noise tiles — all generated in code, no image assets, covered by integrity tests
```

```
24:| Minimap sampling     | `lib/game/render/minimapColors.test.ts`                                     | Top-block detection (water visible), height shading, per-block colors                                                                                                                                                                   |
25:| Renderer logic       | `lib/game/render/{mobVisuals,heldItem,heldItemPose,extrudedSprite}.test.ts` | Model lifecycle (create/reuse/remove/dispose), positioning, bob, item swaps, held-item swing/equip/bob poses, sprite-extrusion face culling and vertex colors — pure Three.js, no DOM                                                   |
12:GameRenderer (lib/game/render/) Three.js: maps simulation state → GPU objects
20:- `useMinecraftGame` creates the `GameEngine` in the canvas mount's callback ref, then an effect builds the `GameRenderer`, the `AudioDirector`, and `inputController` and drives the `requestAnimationFrame` loop: `engine.step(dt, input)` (in bounded catch-up substeps of ≤50 ms, so slow frames — e.g. software GL — don't run the simulation in slow motion) → drain engine events (death/respawn handling, `attackSwung` → `renderer.triggerSwing()`, + `audio.handleEvent`) → `minimap.sync(state)` → `renderer.sync(state)` → `audio.sync(state, dt)` → `renderer.render()`. The minimap must sync **before** the renderer because it reads `state.worldMeshDirty`, which `renderer.sync` clears.
29:- `GameEngine.ts` — owns `GameState`, processes `dispatch(Command)`, advances `step(dt, input)`, serializes saves, and publishes snapshots (`subscribe`/`getSnapshot`). Randomness is injectable (`rng`) and the world size is overridable for fast headless tests.
30:- `state.ts` — `GameState` (player with `yaw`/`pitch`, inventory, mobs as **logical entities with no Three.js objects**, day clock, mining progress, timers) plus `FrameInput`, `GameSnapshot`, `GameEvent`.
33:- `systems/` — one module per mechanic, each a function over `GameState`:
51:- `GameRenderer.ts` — scene/camera/WebGL/lighting; `sync(state)` maps simulation state to visuals each frame; `dispose()` frees every GPU resource. WebGL creation returns `{ ok: false, error }` instead of throwing.
53:- `mobVisuals.ts` — mob id → model map; creates/removes models as mobs spawn/die and animates bob + leg gait from mob state (the simulation knows nothing about legs).
54:- `heldItem.ts` / `crackOverlay.ts` — first-person item model and the 8-stage mining crack box (stage = progress / hardness). Held blocks stay cubes; everything else is the item's 16×16 inventory sprite extruded into a pixel-thick voxel mesh (`extrudedSprite.ts`, vertex colors, silhouette-only side faces) — the render layer imports `lib/ui/spritePixels` for this, which is legal because spritePixels is pure pixel-buffer code with no DOM. The holder group is posed every frame by `heldItemPose.ts` (pure math): a one-shot swing on the `attackSwung` event (the shell calls `renderer.triggerSwing()` from the event drain), a looping swing while mining, a walk bob scaled by horizontal speed, an equip dip on slot switch, and a faint idle sway.
```

id: toolu_01KdPaBn1QikkuVTaj4XHzMa
```
44	7. Night hostile spawning (`systems/spawnDirector.ts`, interval/cap in config)
45	8. Mob AI: wander/aggro/flee, attacks with line-of-sight, daylight burn (`systems/mobAI.ts`)
46	
47	Combat (`systems/combat.ts`) runs on the `attack` command rather than per frame. New mechanics get a new system module and a slot in this sequence — don't grow the engine class with inline logic.
48	
49	## Renderer (`lib/game/render/`)
50	
51	- `GameRenderer.ts` — scene/camera/WebGL/lighting; `sync(state)` maps simulation state to visuals each frame; `dispose()` frees every GPU resource. WebGL creation returns `{ ok: false, error }` instead of throwing.
52	- **World mesh**: one mesh covers the visible region (not chunked), rebuilt when the player crosses a `RENDER_GRID` (20-block) boundary or when the engine sets `state.worldMeshDirty` (block edits, respawn, unstuck). Old geometry is disposed on rebuild.
53	- `mobVisuals.ts` — mob id → model map; creates/removes models as mobs spawn/die and animates bob + leg gait from mob state (the simulation knows nothing about legs).
54	- `heldItem.ts` / `crackOverlay.ts` — first-person item model and the 8-stage mining crack box (stage = progress / hardness). Held blocks stay cubes; everything else is the item's 16×16 inventory sprite extruded into a pixel-thick voxel mesh (`extrudedSprite.ts`, vertex colors, silhouette-only side faces) — the render layer imports `lib/ui/spritePixels` for this, which is legal because spritePixels is pure pixel-buffer code with no DOM. The holder group is posed every frame by `heldItemPose.ts` (pure math): a one-shot swing on the `attackSwung` event (the shell calls `renderer.triggerSwing()` from the event drain), a looping swing while mining, a walk bob scaled by horizontal speed, an equip dip on slot switch, and a faint idle sway.
55	- `minimap.ts` / `minimapColors.ts` — the top-right minimap: pure column sampling (top non-air block, height-shaded `BLOCK_COLORS`) feeding a 2D canvas that rebuilds its 128×128 base only when the player crosses a 16-block grid boundary or `worldMeshDirty` is set (read-only — the renderer owns clearing it), then blits with a yaw-rotated player arrow at ~10 Hz.
56	
57	## Audio (`lib/game/audio/`)
58	
59	All sound is procedural — no audio files, mirroring the sprite system. SFX are synthesized by [ZZFX](https://github.com/KilledByAPixel/ZzFX) parameter arrays; music is a generative ambient pad over raw WebAudio nodes.
60	
61	- `audioDirector.ts` — the shell-side observer, exactly parallel to the renderer: `handleEvent(GameEvent)` plays one-shots (block break/place by material, hurt/eat/jump/land, mob attacks, death/respawn stingers) and `sync(state, dt)` drives everything continuous (footsteps, mining hit ticks, mob ambience, music mood). Music follows `state.daylight` immediately and the biome with 5 s hysteresis, and ducks while paused. The engine never imports this module.
62	- `synth.ts` — `SynthBackend`, **the only seam that touches WebAudio for SFX**: renders each `SoundDef` to an `AudioBuffer` once via `ZZFX.buildSamples` and plays it through gain/pan nodes with playback-rate jitter, a 10-voice cap, and per-sound retrigger throttling (which also de-dupes catch-up substep event bursts). Tests inject a recording fake instead.
63	- `soundParams.ts` / `materials.ts` — pure data: ZZFX parameter tables per `MaterialGroup`/`MobKind` (tune by ear in the [ZZFX designer](https://killedbyapixel.github.io/ZzFX/)) and the exhaustive `BlockId → MaterialGroup` mapping.
64	- `footsteps.ts` / `mobAmbience.ts` / `musicBrain.ts` — pure schedulers/composer (distance-accumulator strides, per-mob randomized call timers with distance gain + look-relative stereo pan, pentatonic random-walk note generation). `musicPlayer.ts` renders notes as detuned pad voices through a lowpass and a procedural convolution reverb.
65	- **Lazy unlock invariant**: no `AudioContext` exists until the first user gesture calls `unlock()` — including the `zzfx` import itself, which instantiates one at module scope and is therefore only ever imported dynamically inside `unlock()`. This satisfies the browser autoplay policy, keeps SSR/`bun test` import-safe, and keeps the console clean for the E2E fixture.
66	- Volume settings (`settings.ts`) persist under their own localStorage key (`minecraft_audio_v1`) — never part of the world save.
67	
68	## Input (`lib/game/input/inputController.ts`)
69	
70	Owns every DOM listener. Continuous input (movement keys, mouse button, pointer lock, CapsLock) is exposed as a `FrameInput` the engine reads each step; discrete actions (hotbar, inventory toggle, place, eat, attack, unstuck, pause, F3 debug) become commands; mouse-look calls `engine.applyLook`. The first click only acquires pointer lock; `KeyI` exits it.
71	
72	**Pause ↔ pointer lock**: the browser consumes Escape to exit pointer lock, so Esc never reaches keydown while locked — losing the lock during plain gameplay is the pause trigger (`pointerlockchange` dispatches `pause`). The inventory (`KeyI`) and death paths set their state flags _before_ the async `pointerlockchange` fires, and the engine's `pause` command additionally ignores those states, so they don't open the menu. While unlocked, Escape toggles pause directly (and closes the inventory). "Back to Game" resumes and re-requests the lock; Chrome's ~1.25 s cooldown after Esc can reject that request — the rejection is swallowed and the player just clicks the canvas.
73	
```

29	## Running the E2E suite
30	
31	```bash
32	bunx playwright install chromium   # once
33	bun run test:e2e                   # builds and starts the prod server itself
34	```
35	
36	E2E tests assert through `window.__monecraft` (the live engine/renderer/input handle — also handy in the browser console) instead of pixels; the one render check uses `renderer.renderedTriangles()`. The shared fixture fails any test that logs a console error.
37	
38	**happy-dom has no 2D canvas context**: generated sprites degrade to a transparent data URL in component tests — assert sprite pixels in the pure `lib/ui` tests and component structure via roles/labels/`data-icon` attributes, not image sources.

> AGENT

Everything is built, verified, and committed locally — but the permission system blocked the push, reading your earlier "create a new branch, all commits on it, then open a pr, what do you think?" as a discussion question rather than authorization. So the branch hasn't left your machine. Here's where things stand:

## Done: V-key camera view toggle (first → third rear → third front)

Six commits on `feature/camera-view-toggle`:

1. **Add distance to voxelRaycast results** — exposes the DDA ray parameter for camera clamping
2. **Add camera view mode state with a V-key cycle command** — engine state + `toggleCameraView`, session-only (never saved)
3. **Extract shared held-item model building** — `itemModel.ts`, pure refactor
4. **Add humanoid player body model with walk and swing animation** — head/torso/arms/legs with joint pivots, walk gait, mining/attack chop, held item in the right hand
5. **Render third-person camera views with wall clamping** — pure pose math in `cameraView.ts`; rear keeps your heading, front flips it and inverts the tilt (authentic F5 feel); the boom raycasts terrain so walls never occlude you; gameplay stays eye-relative in every mode
6. **Document the V camera view toggle** — README, pause-menu controls, CHANGELOG, architecture/testing docs

**Verification — all green:** lint (0 errors), typecheck, format:check, 245 unit tests (24 new), production build, and all 7 Playwright e2e tests including a new camera-cycle test that ran in a real browser.

## To ship it

If you want me to push and open the PR, just say "push and open the PR" (explicit wording gets past the classifier). Or do it yourself with:

```
! git push -u origin feature/camera-view-toggle
! gh pr create --repo hutusi/monecraft --base main
```

One thing worth doing before merge either way: a quick manual pass in the browser (`bun run dev`) — press V while walking, mining, and standing against a wall — since limb proportions and the 4-block boom distance were tuned on paper, not by eye. Constants live in `lib/game/config.ts` (`THIRD_PERSON_DISTANCE`) and `lib/game/render/playerModel.ts` if anything looks off.

> AGENT

CodeRabbit reviewed the PR and left exactly **one finding** (no other inline comments — the rest of its review is just a walkthrough summary):

**🟠 Major — `inputController.ts`: V doesn't work while the game is paused.** The `KeyV` handler sits below the `if (engine.state.paused) return;` early-return, so camera cycling is blocked in the pause menu — even though the engine-side dispatch case was deliberately written unconditional (its comment says "works while dead/paused, like Minecraft F5"). CodeRabbit suggests moving the `KeyV` check above the paused guard.

My assessment: the finding is technically correct — there's a real inconsistency between the engine comment and the input layer. But it's a judgment call, not a bug with one right answer:

- **Accept the fix** (move `KeyV` above the guard): matches real Minecraft, where F-keys work even with the menu open, and makes the code honor the engine comment. The view would visibly change behind the dimmed pause menu — harmless, since the renderer keeps drawing while paused. V already works while dead and with the inventory open, so this would make it work everywhere.
- **Reject it**: this project already blocks F3 behind the same paused guard, so V-blocked-while-paused is consistent with the existing precedent ("only Escape works in the pause menu"). In that case the engine dispatch comment should drop the "paused" claim to stop overpromising.

I'd lean toward accepting it — it's a two-line move, aligns with the Minecraft muscle-memory the feature is imitating, and removes the comment/behavior mismatch rather than papering over it.

Want me to apply the fix (plus a small engine test asserting V works while paused) and commit it locally for you to check before I push?

Perfect! Now I have a complete picture. Let me generate a comprehensive report.

## Structured Summary: Minecraft-like Voxel Game Architecture for Player Skin Customization

### 1. SETTINGS PERSISTENCE PATTERN (Audio Template)

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/audio/settings.ts`

The audio settings module provides the exact pattern you should mirror for skin presets:

- **Storage Key:** `AUDIO_SETTINGS_KEY = "minecraft_audio_v1"` (line 4)
- **Load Function:** `readAudioSettings(storage: Storage = localStorage)` (line 12)
  - Reads from localStorage, returns `DEFAULT_AUDIO_SETTINGS` on missing/corrupt data
  - Uses try-catch to silently fall back to defaults
  - Validates & clamps data with `clamp01()` (line 6-9) — enforces 0..1 range, rejects non-numbers
  - Sanitizes boolean fields: `parsed?.muted === true` (line 20)
  
- **Save Function:** `writeAudioSettings(settings: AudioSettings, storage: Storage = localStorage)` (line 27)
  - Wraps in try-catch with silent failure (line 31-32 comment: "Quota/privacy-mode failures just lose the preference, never the game")
  - Uses `JSON.stringify()` for serialization

**Test File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/audio/settings.test.ts`
- Fakes Storage via Map-backed mock (line 5-16)
- Tests round-trip persistence (line 20-24)
- Tests fallback to defaults on missing/null/invalid JSON (line 26-30)
- Tests out-of-range & wrong-typed field sanitization (line 32-37)

---

### 2. AUDIO SETTINGS UI FLOW (PauseMenu Integration)

**Component:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/PauseMenu.tsx`

**Props Received:**
```typescript
type PauseMenuProps = {
  audioSettings: AudioSettings;
  onAudioSettingsChange: (partial: Partial<AudioSettings>) => void;
  // ... other props
};
```
(lines 4-12)

**Structure:**
- Three sections: action buttons, sound/music sliders + mute button, controls list (lines 49-92)
- Volume sliders (lines 34-47):
  - Input range 0-100, normalized to 0..1 by dividing by 100
  - Calls `onAudioSettingsChange({ [key]: value })` with partial update
  - Clamps to [0, 1] on line 43
- Mute button toggles boolean state (lines 79-81)

**Props Plumbing — from `useMinecraftGame.ts`:**
- Line 74: State holds `audioSettings: AudioSettings`
- Line 90-96: `updateAudioSettings` callback:
  ```typescript
  const updateAudioSettings = useCallback((partial: Partial<AudioSettings>) => {
    const next = { ...audioSettingsRef.current, ...partial };
    audioSettingsRef.current = next;
    setAudioSettings(next);  // React state update
    writeAudioSettings(next);  // localStorage persist
    audioRef.current?.setSettings(next);  // Live AudioDirector update
  }, []);
  ```
- Line 104: PauseMenu receives `onAudioSettingsChange={updateAudioSettings}`

**Test File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/PauseMenu.test.tsx`
- Mock setup (line 7-19): creates props object with mocked callbacks
- Button dispatch tests (line 23-41): verifies callbacks fire
- Volume slider tests (line 57-65): fires `onAudioSettingsChange` with normalized values
- Mute toggle test (line 67-72): verifies boolean flip

---

### 3. PAUSEMENU STRUCTURE & STYLING

**HTML Structure:**
- `.pause-overlay` (fullscreen dimmed backdrop, line 50)
- `.pause-menu` (grid container, line 51)
  - `.pause-title` (line 52)
  - Action buttons (`.mc-button`, lines 53-74)
  - `.pause-sound` section (lines 76-82):
    - Two `.pause-sound-row` labels with range sliders (`.mc-slider`)
    - One mute button
  - `.pause-save-message` (transient feedback, line 83)
  - `.pause-controls` (read-only keybinds table, lines 84-91)

**CSS File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/app/hud.css`
- `.pause-overlay` (lines 3-11): `position: absolute; inset: 0; z-index: 9; display: flex`
- `.pause-menu` (lines 13-18): `display: grid; gap: 8px; width: min(92vw, 320px)`
- `.pause-sound` (lines 71-75): `display: grid; gap: 8px`
- `.pause-sound-row` (lines 77-86): `grid-template-columns: 64px 1fr; align-items: center`
- `.mc-button` (lines 29-63): Minecraft-style beveled buttons with hover/active/disabled states
- `.mc-slider` (lines 88-124): Styled range input with custom webkit/moz thumbs
- Color/font vars: `--mc-text-light`, `--mc-text-shadow`, `--mc-button`, `--mc-button-hover`

---

### 4. HOW GAMERENDERER/PLAYERVISUALS RECEIVES LIVE UPDATES

**GameRenderer Pattern:**
File: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts`

- Constructor creates playerVisuals once (line 86): `this.playerVisuals = createPlayerVisuals(this.scene)`
- `sync(state: GameState, timeMs: number)` called every frame (line 99-112):
  ```typescript
  sync(state: GameState, timeMs: number): void {
    // ...
    this.playerVisuals.sync(state, timeMs);  // Line 110
  }
  ```
- One-shot methods: `triggerSwing()` (lines 119-122) dispatches to playerVisuals
- `dispose()` cleans up (line 131): `this.playerVisuals.dispose()`

**AudioDirector Runtime Update Pattern:**
File: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/audio/audioDirector.ts`

- Constructor (line 105): takes `deps: AudioDirectorDeps` with injectable graph
- State field (line 113): `let settings = DEFAULT_AUDIO_SETTINGS`
- **Runtime setter (line 270-273):**
  ```typescript
  setSettings(next) {
    settings = next;
    applyVolumes();  // Immediately updates audio context gains
  }
  ```
- Called from useMinecraftGame line 95: `audioRef.current?.setSettings(next);`
- Frame loop calls `sync()` (line 202) which uses internal `settings` state

**Mirror Pattern for Player Skins:**
The model is created once in `createPlayerVisuals()` (line 34 of playerVisuals.ts) and stored in closure. To support live skin swaps, you would need to either:
1. Add a **setter method to PlayerVisuals** (like `setPlayerModel(colors: PlayerColors)`) that rebuilds materials in-place
2. Or pass a `playerColors` parameter through `PlayerVisualsState` that `sync()` reads and applies

---

### 5. MOB TEMPLATE PATTERN (Candidate for Skin Presets)

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobs.ts`

**Structure:**
```typescript
export type MobTemplate = {
  speed: number;
  hp: number;
  detectRange: number;
  attackDamage: number;
  attackCooldown: number;
  modelArgs: Parameters<typeof createMobModel>;  // 7-tuple of args
};

export const MOB_TEMPLATES: Record<MobKind, MobTemplate> = {
  sheep: {
    modelArgs: [
      0xf5f5f5,  // bodyColor
      0xd8d8d8,  // headColor
      0xb7b7b7,  // legColor
      0x111111,  // eyeColor
      0xcecece,  // detailColor
      [1.05, 0.75, 1.35],  // bodySize [x, y, z]
      [0.58, 0.48, 0.5]    // headSize [x, y, z]
    ]
  },
  // ... 5 more mob kinds
};
```

**Quote:** Lines 13-62 define all MOB_TEMPLATES; each has 7 modelArgs components.

**Parallel to Player Skins:**
`createPlayerModel()` (playerModel.ts lines 25-31) currently hardcodes 5 color constants:
```typescript
const SKIN = 0xc68e63;    // Line 25
const HAIR = 0x4a3220;    // Line 26
const SHIRT = 0x2e8b83;   // Line 27
const PANTS = 0x3b3f8f;   // Line 28
const SHOES = 0x4a3527;   // Line 29
const EYE_WHITE = 0xffffff;
const EYE_PUPIL = 0x2b2b45;
```

A `PLAYER_SKIN_PRESETS` could follow the same shape:
```typescript
export type PlayerSkinPreset = {
  name: string;
  colors: {
    skin: number;
    hair: number;
    shirt: number;
    pants: number;
    shoes: number;
  };
};
```

---

### 6. REFERENCES TO CREATEPLAYERMODEL & COLOR CONSTANTS

**References to `createPlayerModel`:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerVisuals.ts:6` — import
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerVisuals.ts:34` — instantiate in closure
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerModel.test.ts:3` — import in tests

**References to color constants (SKIN, HAIR, SHIRT, PANTS, SHOES):**
All in playerModel.ts only — **no other file references them** (grep confirmed). This means:
- Colors are **tightly coupled** to the model creation function
- To parameterize them, you'd refactor `createPlayerModel(colors?: PlayerColors)` or create a separate factory

**Test Coverage:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerModel.test.ts` (43 lines)
  - Tests limb pivoting, face orientation, item holder attachment, material/geometry disposal

All architecture claims from the brief verified against the code. Here is the implementation plan.

# Implementation Plan: Player Skin Presets (Appearance)

## Verified ground truth (deltas from the brief: none significant)

- `createPlayerModel()` (`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerModel.ts`) takes no args; 7 color constants; `skinMat` is shared by arms + skull; `HAIR` material is created inline at line 103; eye materials created at 106-107. `materials`/`geometries` arrays only.
- `createPlayerVisuals(scene)` calls `createPlayerModel()` once in a closure; returns `{ sync, triggerSwing, dispose }`.
- `GameRenderer` holds `private readonly playerVisuals` (line 42/86); `triggerSwing()` is the existing one-shot fan-out template.
- `useMinecraftGame` audio pattern confirmed: storage-read effect (lines 83-88) runs in the first commit; the renderer effect runs in a *later* commit (ctx is set by the `attachMount` callback ref → re-render), so refs populated by the load effect are reliably available when the renderer is created. `updateAudioSettings` (90-96) is the callback template. `window.__monecraft` exposes `renderer`.
- Sprite pipeline: `lib/ui/spritePixels.ts` (`SPRITE_SIZE = 16`, `paintGrid(grid, palette)` with `Rgb = [r,g,b]` and `.`/space transparent) → `lib/ui/sprites.ts` `spriteDataUrl(key, () => pixels)` with cache + happy-dom transparent fallback → `components/game/PixelImg.tsx` (hydration-safe `<img>`). `lib/ui` already imports from `lib/game` (`ITEM_DEF_BY_ID`), so a `lib/ui` → `lib/game/playerSkins` import is consistent.
- PauseMenu test style: roles/labels + `aria-pressed` (mute button), `mock()` callbacks. E2E style: `Escape` pauses directly when unlocked; assertions via `__monecraft` and `localStorage` (see `e2e/smoke.e2e.ts` save test reading `minecraft_save_v5`).

---

## New files

### 1. `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/playerSkins.ts` — presets (pure, no imports)

```ts
export type PlayerPalette = {
  skin: number; hair: number; shirt: number; pants: number;
  shoes: number; eyeWhite: number; eyePupil: number;
};
export type SkinPreset = { id: SkinId; label: string; palette: PlayerPalette };
export type SkinId = "default" | "alex" | "zombie" | "skeleton" | "knight" | "robot";

export const DEFAULT_SKIN_ID: SkinId = "default";
export const SKIN_PRESETS: readonly SkinPreset[] = [ ... ];
export function isSkinId(value: unknown): value is SkinId;
export function getSkinPreset(id: string): SkinPreset;   // unknown/corrupt id → default preset
```

The six palettes (default = the current constants, byte-for-byte):

| preset | skin | hair | shirt | pants | shoes | eyeWhite | eyePupil |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Default** | `0xc68e63` | `0x4a3220` | `0x2e8b83` | `0x3b3f8f` | `0x4a3527` | `0xffffff` | `0x2b2b45` |
| **Alex** | `0xe6b18a` | `0xb5602e` | `0x5f8a3a` | `0x6e5036` | `0x57422c` | `0xffffff` | `0x3f6b3a` |
| **Zombie** | `0x6a9a4e` | `0x4e7a3a` | `0x2f6e62` | `0x4f4377` | `0x3d3326` | `0x1d2b1d` | `0x0a120a` |
| **Skeleton** | `0xdcdcd2` | `0xc4c4ba` | `0xb1b1a7` | `0x9a9a90` | `0x83837a` | `0x2e2e2e` | `0x121212` |
| **Knight** | `0xaab2bd` | `0x7c8591` | `0x8d96a3` | `0x646d79` | `0x4a525c` | `0x161a20` | `0x161a20` |
| **Robot** | `0x767f8a` | `0x4d545e` | `0x5b636d` | `0x444b54` | `0x32373e` | `0x35e9ff` | `0x0b6f80` |

Design notes: Knight's eyeWhite == eyePupil intentionally renders a continuous dark visor slit across both eye boxes. Robot's bright cyan eyeWhite reads as a glow at this material roughness; a true `emissive` channel is a deliberate v2 omission (would complicate `setPalette` to color-only swaps). Zombie keeps the MC zombie read: green skin, darker teal shirt than Default, faded purple pants, near-black eyes.

### 2. `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/skinSettings.ts` — persistence (mirror `lib/game/audio/settings.ts` exactly)

```ts
export const SKIN_SETTINGS_KEY = "minecraft_skin_v1";
export type SkinSettings = { skinId: SkinId };           // object, not bare string — forward-compat
export const DEFAULT_SKIN_SETTINGS: SkinSettings = { skinId: DEFAULT_SKIN_ID };
export function readSkinSettings(storage: Storage = localStorage): SkinSettings;  // try/catch, isSkinId-validate, fallback default
export function writeSkinSettings(settings: SkinSettings, storage: Storage = localStorage): void;  // silent catch
```

### 3. `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/ui/skinPortrait.ts` — bust portrait pixels (pure, bun-testable)

```ts
export function renderSkinPortraitPixels(palette: PlayerPalette): Uint8ClampedArray;
```

Implementation: convert hex → `Rgb` (`[(hex>>16)&255, (hex>>8)&255, hex&255]`), derive shades (`shade(rgb, 0.75)` for skin shadow `S` and shirt hem `T`), then `paintGrid(BUST_GRID, {...})`. Suggested 16×16 grid (legend: `h` hair, `s` skin, `S` skin-shadow, `w` eyeWhite, `p` pupil, `t` shirt, `T` shirt-shadow):

```
"................"
"...hhhhhhhhhh..."
"...hhhhhhhhhh..."
"...hhhhhhhhhh..."
"...hssssssssh..."
"...ssssssssss..."
"...sswwsswwss..."
"...sswpsspwss..."
"...ssssssssss..."
"...sssSSSSsss..."
"....SssssssS...."
"......ssss......"
"......SssS......"
".tttttttttttttt."
"tttttttttttttttt"
"TTTTTTTTTTTTTTTT"
```

### 4. Tests: `lib/game/playerSkins.test.ts`, `lib/game/skinSettings.test.ts`, `lib/ui/skinPortrait.test.ts` (details in commit slices).

## Modified files

### `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerModel.ts`

- Delete the 7 module constants; import `DEFAULT_PLAYER_PALETTE` (add it as a named export of `playerSkins.ts` — alias of the default preset's palette) and `type PlayerPalette`.
- Signature: `createPlayerModel(palette: PlayerPalette = DEFAULT_PLAYER_PALETTE): PlayerModel` — zero churn for existing call sites and all 4 existing tests.
- Extend `PlayerModel` with `paletteMaterials: Record<keyof PlayerPalette, THREE.MeshStandardMaterial>` (the same instances also stay in `materials` for disposal — no double-dispose risk, `materials` remains the single disposal authority). Name the inline hair material; keep `material(color)` helper but collect the 7 into the record.
- Add `export function applyPalette(model: PlayerModel, palette: PlayerPalette): void` — `for (const key of keys) model.paletteMaterials[key].color.setHex(palette[key])`. No material/geometry allocation; live-safe.

### `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/playerVisuals.ts`

- Add `setPalette(palette: PlayerPalette): void` to the `PlayerVisuals` type, implemented as `applyPalette(model, palette)`.
- Do **not** add a constructor param (design Q4 decision): the renderer effect calls `setPlayerSkin` synchronously after `GameRenderer.create` and before the first `requestAnimationFrame`, so no rendered frame can show the default palette. Also the body boots invisible (first-person + `visible = false`). Constructor param rejected as churn without observable benefit.

### `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts`

- Add next to `triggerSwing()` (line ~119):

  setPlayerSkin(palette: PlayerPalette): void { this.playerVisuals.setPalette(palette); }
  ```
- Renderer stays id-agnostic (takes a palette, not a `SkinId`); the hook resolves id → palette. Keeps render code decoupled from preference plumbing.

### `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`

Mirror the audio pattern, plus one new ref because skin needs the renderer (audio has `audioRef`; there is no renderer ref today):

- State: `const [skinId, setSkinId] = useState<SkinId>(DEFAULT_SKIN_ID);` + `skinIdRef` + new `rendererRef = useRef<GameRenderer | null>(null)`.
- Extend the existing post-mount storage effect (lines 83-88) — same effect, not a new one: `const { skinId: storedSkin } = readSkinSettings(); skinIdRef.current = storedSkin; queueMicrotask(() => setSkinId(storedSkin));` (renderer doesn't exist yet in this commit — ordering argument above).
- In the renderer effect, right after `canvasRef.current = renderer.domElement;`: `rendererRef.current = renderer; renderer.setPlayerSkin(getSkinPreset(skinIdRef.current).palette);` Cleanup: `rendererRef.current = null;` alongside `canvasRef.current = null`.
- Callback:
  ```ts
  const updateSkin = useCallback((id: SkinId) => {
    skinIdRef.current = id;
    setSkinId(id);
    writeSkinSettings({ skinId: id });
    rendererRef.current?.setPlayerSkin(getSkinPreset(id).palette);
  }, []);
  ```
- Return `skinId, updateSkin`.

export function skinPortraitUrl(skinId: SkinId): string {
  return spriteDataUrl(`skin:${skinId}`, () => renderSkinPortraitPixels(getSkinPreset(skinId).palette));
}
```
(Reuses the cache and the happy-dom transparent-pixel degradation for free.)

Pass `skinId={skinId} onSkinChange={updateSkin}` into `<PauseMenu>` (line ~101) from the hook return.

After `.pause-sound` block (~line 71): `.pause-skins` (grid, gap 8px, margin-top 4px), `.pause-skins-title` (reuse `var(--mc-text-light)` + `var(--mc-text-shadow)`, font-weight 700), `.pause-skins-grid` (`grid-template-columns: repeat(3, 1fr); gap: 6px;`), `.skin-swatch` (column flex, mc-button bevel recipe: `background: var(--mc-button)`, the same 2px `#a8a8a8/#2c2c2c` border-color trick, hover → `var(--mc-button-hover)`), `.skin-swatch[aria-pressed="true"]` (highlight border, e.g. gold `#f0be3c` bevel + brighter background) so the selected state is style-driven off the accessible attribute.

## Commit slices

**Slice 1 — `Add player skin presets and persisted skin selection`**
Files: `lib/game/playerSkins.ts`, `lib/game/skinSettings.ts`, both tests.
Why-body: skins are a render/UI preference like audio volumes, not world state, so they get their own validated localStorage key (`minecraft_skin_v1`) and a presets module both the renderer and React shell can import without touching the engine. Stored as `{ skinId }` so future appearance fields don't need a key migration.
Tests: presets — 6 entries, unique ids, `getSkinPreset("nope")` → default, `DEFAULT_PLAYER_PALETTE` equals the default preset palette. Settings — Map-backed fake `Storage` (copy the shape from `lib/game/audio/settings.test.ts`): roundtrip, missing key → default, corrupt JSON → default, unknown id → default, throwing `setItem` swallowed.

**Slice 2 — `Parameterize the player body palette and recolor it live`**
Files: `playerModel.ts`, `playerVisuals.ts`, `GameRenderer.ts`, `playerModel.test.ts` (+2 tests), `playerVisuals.test.ts` (+1).
Why-body: the body's colors were burned in as module constants; threading a `PlayerPalette` through and exposing the seven materials by name lets a skin change be a set of `color.setHex` calls — no model rebuild, no disposal churn, no per-frame cost.
New tests: `createPlayerModel(zombiePalette)` → `paletteMaterials.skin.color.getHex() === 0x6a9a4e`; `applyPalette` swaps colors on the *same* material instances (capture refs before/after) and allocates nothing (`materials.length` unchanged); `paletteMaterials` values are all contained in `materials`. playerVisuals: `visuals.setPalette(robot)` then assert a mesh material color through the scene. Existing 4 model tests pass untouched (default param).

**Slice 3 — `Generate 16x16 bust portraits for skin presets`**
Files: `lib/ui/skinPortrait.ts`, `lib/ui/skinPortrait.test.ts`, `lib/ui/sprites.ts`.
Why-body: the picker needs a face for each preset; deriving a pixel bust from the same palette that colors the 3D body keeps the zero-asset guarantee and makes the swatch automatically truthful for any future preset.
Tests (mirror `spritePixels.test.ts` integrity style): every preset yields a 16×16×4 buffer with >40 opaque pixels; deterministic; all six pairwise distinct; hair rows (y=1..3) use the hair color and shoulder rows (y=13..14) the shirt color for a known preset.

**Slice 4 — `Add an Appearance picker to the pause menu`**
Files: `PauseMenu.tsx`, `PauseMenu.test.tsx`, `MinecraftGame.tsx`, `useMinecraftGame.ts`, `app/hud.css`, `e2e/smoke.e2e.ts` (or a new `e2e` spec), `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/adding-content.md`.
Why-body: surfaces the presets where players already manage preferences; selection applies live through the renderer (same path audio settings take to the director) and persists outside the world save so it survives resets.
Component tests: six `role=button` matching `/skin$/`; `aria-pressed=true` only on `skinId`; clicking "Robot skin" calls `onSkinChange("robot")` once; default props use `DEFAULT_SKIN_ID`. **No image/pixel assertions** (happy-dom renders transparent data URLs).
E2E (follows the pause-menu/save patterns): `Escape` to pause → `getByRole("button", { name: "Robot skin" })` click → `expect(JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem("minecraft_skin_v1"))).toEqual({ skinId: "robot" })` → reload via the existing boot-wait helper → `Escape` → robot button has `aria-pressed="true"`. Renderer-side pixel/material readback deliberately skipped: it would require a new debug getter for one assertion; the localStorage + persisted-selection + zero-console-error fixture is sufficient signal. (If desired later: a `renderer.playerSkinShirtHex()` debug getter is the cheap extension.)

## Sequencing and risks

Slices are strictly ordered by dependency (1 → 2 and 1 → 3 independent of each other; 4 needs all). Risks: (a) effect-ordering for the boot palette — relies on the same first-commit-loads-refs guarantee audio already depends on, called out in a comment; (b) `SkinId` union vs persisted strings — `isSkinId` guard at the storage boundary keeps strict mode honest; (c) hud.css bevel duplication for `.skin-swatch` — copy the exact `mc-button` border-color values rather than `@extend`-style refactors.

## Verification checklist

1. `bun run lint && bun run typecheck && bun run format:check`
2. `bun test` (new: playerSkins, skinSettings, skinPortrait; extended: playerModel, playerVisuals, PauseMenu)
3. `bun run build`
4. `bun run test:e2e` (renderer + React-shell change → required per AGENTS.md)
5. Manual pass (visual change): press V for third person, Esc → pick each preset, confirm instant recolor, no flash at boot after reload, knight visor and robot eyes read correctly at distance.

import { renderSpritePixels, SPRITE_SIZE } from "./spritePixels";
import { renderHudIconPixels, type HudIconName } from "./hudPixels";

/**
 * DOM side of the sprite system: turns pure pixel buffers into cached data
 * URLs for <img> tags. happy-dom has no 2D canvas context, so everything
 * degrades to a transparent pixel there — components stay testable while the
 * pixel logic itself is covered by the pure-module tests.
 */

export const TRANSPARENT_PIXEL = "data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7";

const cache = new Map<string, string>();

export function spriteDataUrl(key: string, pixels: () => Uint8ClampedArray): string {
  const hit = cache.get(key);
  if (hit) return hit;

  let url = TRANSPARENT_PIXEL;
  if (typeof document !== "undefined") {
    const canvas = document.createElement("canvas");
    canvas.width = SPRITE_SIZE;
    canvas.height = SPRITE_SIZE;
    const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
    if (ctx) {
      // Copy into a fresh buffer: ImageData requires a non-shared ArrayBuffer.
      ctx.putImageData(new ImageData(new Uint8ClampedArray(pixels()), SPRITE_SIZE, SPRITE_SIZE), 0, 0);
      url = canvas.toDataURL();
    }
  }
  cache.set(key, url);
  return url;
}

/** Data URL for an inventory item's 16x16 icon. */
export function itemIconUrl(itemId: string): string {
  return spriteDataUrl(`item:${itemId}`, () => renderSpritePixels(itemId));
}

/** Data URL for a HUD icon (hearts, drumsticks, armor). */

function persistGame(api: GameApi, onMessage: (text: string) => void): void {
  try {
    writeSave(SAVE_KEY, api.serialize());
    onMessage("Saved");
  } catch {
    onMessage("Save failed");
  }
}

export function useMinecraftGame() {
  const [ctx, setCtx] = useState<GameContext | null>(null);
  const [locked, setLocked] = useState(false);
  const [saveMessage, setSaveMessage] = useState("");
  const [rendererError, setRendererError] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [audioSettings, setAudioSettings] = useState<AudioSettings>(DEFAULT_AUDIO_SETTINGS);
  const canvasRef = useRef<HTMLCanvasElement | null>(null);
  const minimapNodeRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement | null>(null);
  const audioRef = useRef<AudioDirector | null>(null);

  const audioSettingsRef = useRef(audioSettings);

  // Persisted volumes load after mount: render never touches localStorage
  // (SSR), and the setState hops a microtask like the renderer-error report.
  useEffect(() => {
    const stored = readAudioSettings();
    audioSettingsRef.current = stored;
    audioRef.current?.setSettings(stored);
    queueMicrotask(() => setAudioSettings(stored));
  }, []);

  const updateAudioSettings = useCallback((partial: Partial<AudioSettings>) => {
    const next = { ...audioSettingsRef.current, ...partial };
    audioSettingsRef.current = next;
    setAudioSettings(next);
    writeAudioSettings(next);
    audioRef.current?.setSettings(next);
  }, []);

  // Callback ref: the engine boots as soon as the canvas mount exists. A ref
  // callback runs during commit, where side effects and setState are legal.
  const attachMount = useCallback((node: HTMLDivElement | null) => {
    if (!node) {
      setCtx(null);
      return;
    }
    setCtx({ engine: new GameEngine({ save: readSave(SAVE_KEY) }), node });
  }, []);

  // below picks it up lazily once both exist.
  const attachMinimap = useCallback((node: HTMLDivElement | null) => {
    minimapNodeRef.current = node;
  }, []);

  const engine = ctx?.engine ?? null;
  const subscribe = useMemo(() => engine?.subscribe ?? noopSubscribe, [engine]);

  const getServerSnapshot = useCallback(() => PRE_MOUNT_SNAPSHOT, []);
  const snapshot = useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, getSnapshot, getServerSnapshot);

    setSaveMessage(text);
    window.setTimeout(() => setSaveMessage(""), durationMs);
  }, []);

    if (!ctx) return;
